## PDF page 6
Preface






This is the 15th yearly report on Research Publications and Research Profiles for the Faculty
of Medicine & Health Sciences, United Arab Emirates University. This volume is a continua-
tion of a series of annual reports, dating back to our first report in 1991.


As usual, every year, we also try to upgrade the quality of this directory and this year is no
exception. We have continued to include the Departmental Profiles and the Reports from
the Research Priority Groups. We also included short reports on the major conferences this
year; the Neuroscience meeting and the 4th Scientific Conference for Medical Students in
the GCC countries that was held in Muscat. Also included is a brief summary of the work
performed by Karen Neves and Wim Lammers on the biomedical research publication re-
cord in the Emirates in the past years.


As always, it is a pleasure to thank those individuals, who have put this report together, to
cater to the individual layouts and to consolidate all the sections into one book. Ms Shaikha
Al Mazrouei and Mrs Ivanna Lizarriturri ably performed that work. Mr Rajagopalan, as al-
ways, took care of editing the initial departmental contribution and the communication be-
tween all parties. Mr Ashook took numerous pictures and Mr Mohamed Zaggar & Adnan
Tahir took care of the translation.




Professor Wim Lammers                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     Professor M Lukic
              Editor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       Associate Dean for Research

## PDF page 8
Dean's Message




Dear Colleagues,

Once again it is my privilege to thank and commend our faculty and technical staf f          for a
very high level of research productivity during the calendar year 2005. Thanks also to W  im
Lammers for editing all of           the diverse infor mation about our grants and publications into a
cohesive document that hopefully will enable the stor  y of           FMHS r esearch ef forts to be com-
municated ef fectively to all those with an inter est. You, the r eader, can assist us by telling us
what you like and what you don’t like as we strive continually to impr ove this annual r eport.

Grant sponsorship at FMHS involves a wide range of          benefactors, without whom the re-
search could not be conducted. Despite budgetar y constraints, inter nal funding at FMHS
and UAE University happily remains a priority, vital to the attraction and retention of
high quality investigators. The valued contributions of             exter nal bodies such as Sheikh Ham-
dan  Awards for Medical Science, Ter r y Fox Cancer Funds in Dubai and Abu Dhabi,
UAE Red Cr escent, Michael J Fox Foundation and the Wellcome Foundation ar e gr eatly
appr eciated.

Research Conferences on Diabetes and Neurosciences brought inter national scientists of
gr eat statur e to Al Ain and wer e ver  y well attended. Congratulations to the or ganisers!  An
important sequel to the Neur osciences meeting was a visit by Inter national Brain Or ganisa-
tion Pr esident Pr ofessor Albert Aguayo who invited the or ganisers to apply for membership
in this pr estigious body. I am delighted to r eport that the application has been successful. We
wish our Oncolog y Research Gr oup equal success in planning their upcoming confer ence on
Advances in Cancer.

The Master of          Medical Science degree program has been accepted and should enhance re-
search activities in both Medical Micr obiolog y & Immunolog y and Phar macolog y & Toxi-
colog y. Subsequent developments will hopefully include the extension of          the MMedSc to in-
clude Neurosciences and Genetics which have great potential. Can a PhD program be far
behind?

Student research remains a distinguishing feature of         this Medical College. Our dedicated
student body with their research mentors should be suitably proud of        their considerable
achievements. W hether ‘basic science’ or ‘clinical’, ‘simple’ or ‘complex’, the challenge of
taking a research question and attempting its solution is a ver  y special lear ning experience
for a futur e physician.

None of           this would be possible without the dedicated commitment of           UAE University Re-

It is the challenge of         all good biomedical research to enhance the quality of         patient care
thr ough impr oved diagnosis or better tr eatment. We must strive to enhance our clinical inter-
face and to str engthen partnerships within and outside UAE if            we ar e to achieve this objec-
tive. We welcome the gr owth of         Har vard Medical Inter national in Dubai and the recent
ar rival of          Johns Hopkins Inter national in Al Ain as potential allies in our research en-
deavours.

                                                                  Treatment of Peptic Ulceration
                                                                  Leptin is produced by fat cells (adipocytes), and is
                                                                  involved in the control of food intake and energy
                                                                  metabolism in humans. Recently, the role of Leptin
                                                                  as a growth factor has been appreciated, and our
                                                                  study investigates its role on the rate of healing of
                                                                  gastric ulcers in rats because some growth factors
                                                                  are known to protect the gastric mucosa.

MUSCLE
The dynamics of the control of the muscles is an-
other focus of activity involving electrophysiology
and mathematical modelling, and seeks to explain
the influence of various physiological and patho-
logical conditions on changes in muscle perform-
ance. In particular, the effects of chronic ischemia
and muscle fatigue are currently being investigated.
The model of chronic ischemia induced in vivo by                    a
femoral artery occlusion is employed; the effects
of exogenously applied Neuropeptide-Y (NPY) are
of interest because the angiogenesis and revascu-
larisation it can induce are used as tools to alter
the dynamic properties of skeletal muscle.
Changes in muscle dynamics are estimated by
monitoring various parameters i.e. EMG, twitch
force, force/time integral, etc. during various non-
fatiguing and fatiguing protocols induced by electri-
cal stimulation of muscle nerves and ventral spinal
roots. The results are relevant for better under-
standing of changes of intra-muscle dynamics in                      b
various conditions and probing the possibility that
NPY may be a potential drug in treatment of                           Figure 1:Telemetry system (a) and typical ECG data (b)
ischemic disease.

DIABETES AND HEART FUNCTION
Cardiovascular complications are the most com-
mon causes of morbidity and mortality in diabetic
patients. Diabetic cardiomyopathy is associated
with a variety of cardiac dysfunctions, which are
independent of vascular complications. We are
currently investigating the mechanisms that under-
lie contractile defects in cardiac muscle in various
experimental models of diabetes. Much of our
research is conducted in cardiac myocytes isolated
from different regions of the heart. Recent pro-
jects have included an investigation of contraction
and Ca2+ transport in myocytes from C57BL/6J
mice which have a genetic susceptibility to develop
diabetes when fed a high-energy diet. We have
also investigated the effects of multiple low-dose
or single high-dose streptozotocin on contraction
and Ca2+ transport in ventricular myocytes from
diabetes resistant and susceptible rats.  The recent               Figure 2 : Connexin-43 labeling in a rat ventricular myocyte.

bling us to remotely monitor the acute and chronic
effects of diabetes on electrical activity of the heart            SMOOTH MUSCLE MAPPING

## PDF page 64
this year with several major publications. One
example of that is shown in the accompanying
figures. The goal of this project was to determine
the mechanism of pendular contractions, which
are contractions that are notorious for sudden
changes in rhythm and direction. The approach
we chose was to record simultaneously the elec-
trical and mechanical activities in isolated seg-
ments of rabbit ileum.

Figure 1 shows the method used. Electrical activi-
ties were recorded with a row of 32 extracellular
electrodes. Contractions were visualized by plac-
ing 20-40 soot markers on the surface of the seg-
ment and recording their displacements with a
digital video camera. Separate softwares were
written to analyze the electrical and the mechani-
cal activities. Tracings of these are shown in figure
2 in which the upper panel plots the electrical
signal and the lower the mechanical tracings.

In addition, the Faculty Seminar on May 23, 2005
was organized by our Research Priority Group. On
this occasion, Professor Mike Ellis gave a seminar
entitled "Potential for immunomanipulation in
managing infectious complications in haematologi-
cal malignancy: focus on recombinant interleukin
11", and Dr. Tibor Pal presented a lecture on
"Extended spectrum ß-lactamase producing bacte-
ria - the local experience".

Also, several members of the Group gave presen-
tations at the 6th Annual U.A.E. University Re-
search Conference, Al Ain, April 24-26, 2005.

Due to the emergence of the H5N1 “bird flu” vi-
rus Professor Nowotny gave several seminars on
this topic at various hospitals.

The most outstanding event organized by the Re-
search Priority Group “Immunoregulation and Infec-
tion” in 2005, however, was the organization of an
International Conference on Emerging Infectious
Diseases, which was held under the Patronage of
H.E. Sheikh Nahayan Mabarak Al-Nahayan, Minis-
ter of Education, from February 26 to March 01,
2005 at the Intercontinental Resort Al Ain. Fifteen
highly distinguished international speakers gave
keynote presentations on all areas of emerging
infectious diseases, and approx. 720 scientists,
among them many from Europe and the United
States, participated in the Conference. Details
about the Conference and some photos have al-
ready been published in the Research Publications
booklet for 2004 on pages 93 - 95. This Confer-
